# NOTES.md -- ccc8: the Criminal Code (Canada), R.S.C., 1985, c. C-46, lines 4118-5031 (sections 197 to 269.1)

Status (2026-11-13, part agent `ccc8`, run `dst-ccc8`): written after the modules and the seat; every number below was produced by a command run in this session. (The scripts named below are in `work/`, beside the deposit folder, and are not shipped.)
The target is the stable `l4` (1.6.110, `main`, e51181d, 2026-11-11) checked against a second stable build (1.6.109, `main`, 4c887e0, 2026-11-04): `check.sh` runs every module with both and compares the four counts.
Verification, by kind: `check.sh` (counts per module, both builds, last section of this file); `srcrefs.py check --strict` over every layer-1 module and test file (quotations and `@ref` locators against the text, 0 problems); `work/coverage.py` (every non-blank line of the range is in exactly one coverage row, and no row is pending); both stable builds run over every test file that holds a `#TRACE` (7 files, 29 traces) and their complete outputs compared (0 differing lines), because `check.sh` does not see a different trace outcome.
Not verified: nothing here is claimed verified against the official text, which is not among the sources; the expected values were written from the text by one agent, not by an independent reader.
The text is a plain-text rendering, made by a converter script, of the Justice Laws Website consolidation (current to 2026-09-30). It is not the official text. The French version is equally authoritative and is not among the sources.
Where `inputs/PLAN.md` and the brief differ the brief governs: PLAN.md counts 98 section headings for this range and the text has 97 (finding F-2); PLAN.md names `s 2 justice system participant`, `s 2 criminal organization offence` and `terrorism offence` as things s 231 turns on, and the text of s 231 uses none of the three (checked by `grep` over lines 4519-4560; see Seams).

## What is encoded and what is not

Lines 4118-5031: Part VII (disorderly houses, gaming and betting, sections 197 to 213) and the first third of Part VIII (offences against the person, sections 214 to 269.1).
Every provision of the range has a row in the coverage table below and one disposition; nothing is `out-of-scope` or `deferred`.

Layer 1 follows the text's own structure; every rule carries an `@ref` with a `src:` locator and, directly above it, the quotation of the lines it encodes (`-- src:N | text`, produced from the text by a script and checked by `srcrefs.py`).
Every offence has the triple `s N the person commits the offence ...` (a `MAYBE BOOLEAN`), `s N the class of the offence` (`Class of offence`) and `s N the punishment` (`LIST OF Punishment`; a summary limb takes the seam input `s 787(1) the general penalty for an offence punishable on summary conviction`). Regulative provisions (duties of a peace officer, a court, a practitioner, a person who must guard an opening) are written as `DEONTIC` rules and run with `#TRACE`, with a boolean twin for the assertions.
The keel's three files (`ccc-core.l4`, `ccc-definitions.l4`, `ccc-interpretation.l4`) are read-only copies and are unchanged.

## Seat

The seat is a person who is, or may be, accused of an offence under the Criminal Code (the accused), asking on facts they can state whether what they did is an offence, how serious it is and what it can cost, what defences, exemptions and onuses the text gives them, and what the text lets be done to them or their property (arrest without a warrant, search, seizure, forfeiture, sentence) (assumed, not ruled: Singapore's Penal Code is the model, as the maintainer instructed on 2026-11-09, but this is a plain seat layer; no charge-generator contract).
It is `ccc8-seat.l4`, with its cases in `ccc8-cases-seat.l4`.
Every question begins "Under section N," with the Code's own numbering and carries `@export` and plain `@desc` inputs worded as the person would be asked; the questions are grouped under 18 `§` headings in the text's order, and a heading whose group only arises in a situation (a search, a death, a birth, medical assistance in dying) says so in a comment so that a form can ask "Has this happened to you?" first.
Provisions that address only an official (a justice, a court, a judge, a peace officer, the Minister) appear only as what they mean to the person: for example "Under section 236(2), must the court consider a sentence of imprisonment for life for my manslaughter?"; there is no seat for an official.
The question counts are in the file table above; nothing in this file restates a rule, because each question calls a layer-1 function.

How the seat takes its inputs (all of this is assumed, not ruled, and is also written at the head of `ccc8-seat.l4`):

- **Section 2 terms are built from facts.** "Firearm", "bodily harm", "weapon", "intimate partner", "peace officer", "public officer", "Canadian Forces", "common-law partner" and "newly-born child" are computed by calling the keel's `s 2 ...` functions on the facts they ask, never asked as a bare yes or no.
- **Another part's answer is one yes-or-no fact.** A criminal organization under s 467.1(1), a terrorist activity under s 83.01(1), violence under s 3.01, forgery under s 366 and a document under s 321 are answered by other parts; the question asks one yes-or-no fact whose `@desc` says what that section requires, and passes it on as `JUST TRUE` or `JUST FALSE`.
- **Another section of this range is also one yes-or-no fact**, where the facts it needs are many: "is it culpable homicide?", "is it murder?", "did you commit the offence in section 247(1)?". The `@desc` points to the question under that section. Where the facts are few the seat builds the answer instead (criminal negligence, a common gaming house, the duties of section 215(1), the criteria of section 241.2(1)). A person who answers yes to such a fact has made the "not covered" of the earlier question into a definite answer; that is the price of keeping each question short.
- **Gravity and penalty.** For each offence there are two questions, whether the person has committed it and how serious it is and what it can cost. The second returns a record with the class of the offence, the punishments the section itself states (the indictable limb, taken from the layer-1 constants, or the whole punishment where the offence is indictable only) and a flag that section 787(1) (outside this part, encoded by another part) also supplies a penalty on summary conviction. The seat does not guess that penalty: it is a seam (see Seams below).
- **Dates and counts.** An earlier conviction is asked as the section number of the earlier offence (and the subsection, if any), the two conviction dates and the days in custody; a "second offence" is asked as the number of earlier convictions (fork F2).
- **The questions the seat does not ask.** Every layer-1 function that no seat question calls is listed in the next paragraph; the list is a script's output over the seat file, not a recollection.

### Seat audit of 2026-11-12

Every seat question was read, name and `@export` text, against the layer-1 function its body calls (201 questions). 15 did not ask what the function answers: 8 by direction (the name asks one way, the function answers the other, so JUST TRUE or JUST FALSE read the wrong way round) and 7 by subject (the name carries a limb, premise or consequence the function does not compute, or asks what will happen where the function states a duty). Each was retitled to ask what the function answers, in the form "Under section N, ...", and the answer was never negated: a bar's NOTHING and JUST FALSE are not a grant. The facts of every case are unchanged and so are the expected values of every case over a retitled question; each assertion over a retitled question carries a `-- RECONCILED 2026-11-12: was ...` comment in `ccc8-cases-seat.l4`; the two cases over `s 199(3) ... may declare the thing forfeited` (listed, fork F11) and `s 207(1)(b)` (outside the sweep) carry one too, with their values unchanged.

### Name-test sweep of 2026-11-12

The sweep read every layer-1 function whose name contains "may", "permitted", "power", "entitled" or "authorized": 26 of the 378 layer-1 functions, found by a script over the module templates. It applied three tests: (1) a function named for a grant or a power that answers JUST FALSE because an "if" gate failed is flipped to NOTHING, with one fork line (F12, F13, F14) and the marginal reading M7; (2) JUST FALSE is kept where the text closes the gate itself ("only if", "may not ... unless", a closed list), for a bar, for a duty, and for the provision's own question; (3) grants gated by "where", "when" or "upon", or an equivalent ("in the case of"), and "entitled" functions are not flipped but listed in the fork register (F11 and the lead-in of F14), ASSUMED, NOT RULED. Fourteen of the 26 already answer NOTHING when their gate fails and were not touched. The seat questions inherit the answer of the layer-1 function they call and have no gate of their own, so they are not counted.

| flipped to NOTHING | kept JUST FALSE (gate closed by the text) | listed: "may" with where/when/upon | listed: "entitled" |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 3 | 8 | 2 | 0 |

(`s 241.2(3.5)` is counted in the first column and in the third: its "if" paragraphs are flipped and its "In the case of" lead-in is listed, so the four counts add to 13 for 12 functions.)

Flipped to NOTHING (test 1, an "if" gate on a power):
- `s 204(9.1) the Minister may designate the zone, which is deemed to be part of the race-course` (F12)
- `s 241.2(3.2) the practitioner may administer a substance without meeting the requirement of paragraph (3)(h)` (F13)
- `s 241.2(3.5) the practitioner may administer a substance to cause the death of that person` (F14), for the "if" paragraphs only

Kept JUST FALSE (test 2); the reason is the text's own wording or the kind of function, not a failed "if" on a grant:
- `s 199(3) the forfeited thing shall be disposed of or dealt with as the Attorney General may direct`: named for a duty ("shall be disposed of"), not a grant; the contrary-cause gate belongs to the duty.
- `s 199(4) a declaration or direction may be made under subsection 199(3)`: the text closes the gate itself ("No declaration or direction shall be made ... until").
- `s 199(6) this section or section 489 authorizes the seizure, forfeiture or destruction of the equipment`: a bar ("Nothing in this section or in section 489 authorizes").
- `s 207(5) section 207 authorizes the making or recording of the bets`: a bar ("nothing in this section shall be construed as authorizing ... other than in accordance with section 204").
- `s 241.2(1) the person may receive medical assistance in dying`: "only if".
- `s 241.2(5) the person may act as an independent witness`: JUST FALSE comes from an exception that is met in the text's own words ("except if they ..."), not from a failed gate.
- `s 241.2(5.1) the person is permitted to act as an independent witness despite paragraphs (5)(c) and (d)`: the same, "except for" the two practitioners.
- `s 263.1(2) proceedings under subsection (1) may be commenced in that mode`: a bar ("No proceedings under subsection (1) shall be commenced by way of ...").

Listed, not flipped, "may" with where/when/upon or an equivalent (test 3(i); one row each in the fork register):
- `s 199(3) the court, judge, justice or provincial court judge may declare the thing forfeited` (F11): "Except where otherwise expressly provided by law".
- `s 241.2(3.5) the practitioner may administer a substance to cause the death of that person` (F14), for the lead-in only: "In the case of a person who loses the capacity ... after self-administering".

Listed, not flipped, "entitled": none; no function in this range is named "entitled".

Outside the sweep, named "it is lawful" (the coordinator's ruling of 2026-11-12). A function named "it is lawful ..." is a permission but contains none of the sweep's words, so its answer is not flipped: JUST FALSE stays where the condition fails (the closest ruling is (b) of the 2026-11-05 ruling on defences: an unmet condition gives JUST FALSE, read as "this section gives no defence on these facts", never as "unlawful"). A name that does not say which provision makes the thing lawful would let a FALSE read as "unlawful", so the name is scoped to the provision. The other reading, for a lawyer, is NOTHING on a failed gate (the permission is not made and the thing is not said to be unlawful); three of the eight answer JUST FALSE on a failed gate (`s 207(1)(b)` "if the proceeds ... are used for a charitable or religious object or purpose", `s 207(1)(d)` "if (i) ... and (ii) ...", `s 207.1(1)` "when all of the following conditions are satisfied") and the other five already answer NOTHING. None of the eight had a name scoped to its provision, so none is kept as it was; all eight are retitled.

- it is lawful, scoped: 0
- it is lawful, unscoped and retitled: 8 (the name now begins "s N makes it lawful ..."; the answer is unchanged)
- also retitled to the same form, for consistency, though they are inside the sweep and already answer NOTHING (their names contain "may" and "authorized"): 2, `s 207(1)(e)` and `s 207(1)(f)`

The questions that no seat question calls (128 of the 378 layer-1 functions; the rest are called by at least one seat question, directly or inside another call). The reasons are: it is a regulative rule (a `DEONTIC`, which a question cannot return), a definition whose answer another question already builds in its own inputs, a rule addressed only to an official, a function of another section that a question calls inside a longer expression, or a rule left out for cost (the long safeguards of s 241.2(3.1), which have eighteen inputs, and the exemptions of s 207(1)(a) and (c) to (h) for provinces and fair boards). ## Fork register (ccc8)

Every site where the text can be read two or more ways with different outcomes. Each entry: the provision and function, the readings, the one taken, and the text that licenses each. A human reviewer overrules a fork by changing one function; the readings are written beside the function in layer 1.

- F1. s 199(4)(b), "the expiration of thirty days from the time of seizure". Readings: (i) the thirty days run from the day of seizure with the first day excluded and the last included (the general rule of counting days, which is outside this text), so the period expires at the end of day 30 and a declaration may be made from day 31; (ii) the thirty days run from the time of seizure to the same time on day 30, so a declaration may be made on day 30 after that time. Taken: (i) (`s 199(4)(b) thirty days from the time of seizure have expired`: seizure plus 31 days). Licensed by: "expiration of thirty days from the time of seizure" (the text gives no counting rule). Outcome differs only on day 30.
- F2. ss 202(2), 203(d) to (f), "a first offence", "a second offence", "each subsequent offence". Readings: (i) count the person's earlier CONVICTIONS for an offence under the section: none is a first offence, one a second, two or more a subsequent; (ii) count the offences committed, whether or not convicted. Taken: (i), the input being `the number of earlier convictions of the person for an offence under section 202 (203)`. Licensed by: the text does not define "offence" in a series; a limb that raises a minimum is read as turning on a conviction (as the Code does expressly in ss 239(2), 244(3), 244.2(4), where "earlier convicted" is the test).
- F3. s 204(6), "any odd cents over any multiple of five cents in the amount calculated in accordance with the regulations to be payable in respect of each dollar bet". Readings: (i) the odd cents are an amount in dollars for the pool, added to the percentage of the total bet; (ii) the odd cents are counted per dollar bet and multiplied by the dollars bet. Taken: (i), with the amount supplied by the caller, because the calculation is left to regulations outside the sources (`s 204(6) the association may deduct and retain the amount`). Licensed by: "a percentage not exceeding the percentage prescribed by the regulations plus any odd cents".
- F4. ss 207(4), 207(4.01), 207.1(4), "other than ...", "does not include ...". Readings: (i) the exclusion closes the definition: a case that is described but is one of the excluded cases is not a lottery scheme (or slot machine) and the answer is JUST FALSE; (ii) the exclusion only withholds the label and the answer for an excluded case is NOTHING. Taken: (i). Licensed by: "means ... other than" and "means ... It does not include", both definitions that say "means" (the text's own no).
- F5. s 207.1(1)(c), "the lottery scheme is not operated within five nautical miles of a Canadian port at which the ship calls or is scheduled to call". Readings: (i) a distance of exactly five nautical miles is "within", so the condition is met only beyond five; (ii) exactly five is not within. Taken: (i) (distance GREATER THAN 5). Licensed by: ordinary use of "within" as including the stated limit; the text does not say.
- F6. s 215(4)(c), "failed for a period of one month". Readings: (i) the month is complete on the corresponding date of the next month (15 January to 15 February; from 31 January to the last day of February), via daydate `add months`; (ii) the month is complete a day later (the first day excluded, the last included). Taken: (i). Licensed by: "a period of one month" with no counting rule in the text (the Interpretation Act, which would settle it, is not among the sources).
- F7. ss 241.2(3), (3.1), (7), (8), "the medical practitioner or nurse practitioner must (a) ...". A bare "must" not done. Readings: (i) the decision function `the requirements have been met` answers JUST FALSE when a requirement is not met (the "must" is read as the text's own no to a non-complying practitioner), with the duty also written as a regulative rule; (ii) the function answers NOTHING when a requirement is not met, because "must" says what the practitioner has to do and not what follows if they do not (s 241.3 supplies the consequence for a knowing failure). Taken: (i) (decided case by case, the maintainer 2026-11-05). Licensed by: "must" with a list of conditions that read as the conditions of a lawful provision of MAID.
- F8. s 241.3, "knowingly fails to comply ... with all of the requirements set out in paragraphs 241.2(3)(b) to (h) or paragraphs 241.2(3.1)(b) to (k), as the case may be, and with subsection 241.2(8)". Readings: (i) a knowing failure to comply with any of the requirements is the offence; (ii) the offence needs a failure to comply with every one of them ("all of the requirements"). Taken: (i). Licensed by: "fails to comply ... with all of the requirements" read as failing to comply with the set; (ii) would make a practitioner who skipped only the witness requirement innocent, which the heading "Failure to comply with safeguards" does not suggest.
- F9. s 241.2(3.1)(i), "at least 90 clear days between the day on which the first assessment ... begins and the day on which medical assistance in dying is provided". "Clear days" excludes both end days, so the days between are the difference of the two dates less one; a different count (inclusive of one end day) moves the first lawful day by one. Taken: clear days = (provided date minus first-assessment date) minus 1. Licensed by: the words "clear days" (an ordinary legal term, not defined in the text).
- F10. ss 239(2), 244(3), 244.2(4), "10 years have elapsed between the day on which the person was convicted of the earlier offence and the day on which the person was convicted of the offence for which sentence is being imposed, not taking into account any time in custody". Readings: (i) the ten years run from the day of the earlier conviction (calendar years, `add years`) and each day in custody between the two convictions postpones their end by one day, so the earlier offence stops counting on the first day on which the date of the present conviction is not before (earlier conviction date plus ten years plus the custody days); (ii) "elapsed" requires the present conviction to fall AFTER that date. Taken: (i) (the day on which exactly ten years and the custody days have passed counts as "elapsed"). Licensed by: "10 years have elapsed between the day ... and the day ..."; the text does not say whether the end day is included.
- F11. s 199(3), "Except where otherwise expressly provided by law, a court, judge, justice or provincial court judge before whom anything that is seized under this section is brought may declare that the thing is forfeited". LISTED, NOT FLIPPED (name-test sweep of 2026-11-12, test 3(i)): ASSUMED, NOT RULED. `s 199(3) the court, judge, justice or provincial court judge may declare the thing forfeited` is named for a power and answers JUST FALSE where another law expressly provides otherwise, a gate of the kind "except where". Readings: (i) JUST FALSE (ruling (b) of "Periods, where and scope limits": the "where" of the exception is the text's own no); (ii) NOTHING (the Guide's line on grants: the power is limited in its own wording and the case falls outside the limit, so the subsection says nothing about it). Taken: (i). A question for the maintainer or a lawyer, not one to settle by sweep.
- F12. s 204(9.1), "the Minister of Agriculture and Agri-Food may designate, with respect to any race-course, a zone that shall be deemed to be part of the race-course, if (a) ... (b) ... and (c) ...". Flipped by the name-test sweep of 2026-11-12, test 1: a function named for a power whose "if" gate fails. Readings: (i) `s 204(9.1) the Minister may designate the zone ...` answers NOTHING when a condition of (a) to (c) fails, because JUST FALSE would read as "the Minister may not designate"; (ii) it answers JUST FALSE (a failed "if" read as the text's own no, M2). Taken: (i). Licensed by: the Guide, section 5, "In short": NOTHING where the text limits a grant in its own wording and the case falls outside the limit.
- F13. s 241.2(3.2), "For the purposes of subsection (3), the medical practitioner or nurse practitioner may administer a substance to a person to cause their death without meeting the requirement set out in paragraph (3)(h) if (a) ... (b) ... (c) ... and (d) ...". Flipped by the name-test sweep of 2026-11-12, test 1: a function named for a power whose "if" gate fails. Readings: (i) `s 241.2(3.2) the practitioner may administer a substance without meeting the requirement of paragraph (3)(h)` answers NOTHING when a condition fails (the exception is not made and paragraph (3)(h) stays a requirement, which s 241.2(3) answers), because JUST FALSE would read as "the practitioner may not"; (ii) it answers JUST FALSE (a failed "if" read as the text's own no, M2). Taken: (i). Licensed by: as F12.
- F14. s 241.2(3.5), "In the case of a person who loses the capacity to consent to receiving medical assistance in dying after self-administering a substance, provided to them under this section, so as to cause their own death, a medical practitioner or nurse practitioner may administer a substance to cause the death of that person if (a) ... (b) ... and (c) ...". Split by the name-test sweep of 2026-11-12: `s 241.2(3.5) the practitioner may administer a substance to cause the death of that person` is named for a power, and its gate has two kinds. FLIPPED (test 1): when the lead-in is met and an "if" paragraph fails, the function answers NOTHING, because JUST FALSE would read as "the practitioner may not"; the other reading is JUST FALSE (a failed "if", M2). LISTED, NOT FLIPPED (test 3(i)), ASSUMED, NOT RULED: when the lead-in "In the case of a person who loses the capacity ... after self-administering" is not met, the function still answers JUST FALSE ("in the case of" is taken to go with "where" and "when"); the other reading is NOTHING (the Guide's line on grants). Taken: both as stated. The lead-in arm is a question for the maintainer or a lawyer, not one to settle by sweep.
- F15. s 233, "A female person commits infanticide when by a wilful act or omission she causes the death of her newly-born child, if at the time of the act or omission she is not fully recovered from the effects of giving birth to the child and by reason thereof or of the effect of lactation consequent on the birth of the child her mind is then disturbed". Raised by the independent reader of 2026-11-16, who expected NOTHING for a man (the subject "A female person" read as a scope limit with no "only", ruling (c) of 2026-11-05, and M1). Readings: (i) `s 233 the female person commits infanticide` answers JUST FALSE for a person who is not a female person, as it does for a woman whose "when" or "if" gate fails (M2); (ii) it answers NOTHING for the person who is not a female person and JUST FALSE only for the woman whose gate fails. Taken: (i), the one site that differs from M1 (marginal reading M8). Why this site differs: s 233 is not the offence but the definition of the category "infanticide", which `s 231 the kind of homicide offence` and `s 234 the culpable homicide is manslaughter` consume, and both need a definite JUST FALSE ("is not infanticide") to conclude manslaughter. Measured on 1.6.110 on 2026-11-16: with infanticide JUST FALSE, `s 234` answers JUST TRUE and `s 231` answers JUST manslaughter; with infanticide NOTHING, each answers NOTHING. A caller cannot tell a NOTHING from the sex limit from a NOTHING from the undefined "newly-born child" (section 2), so reading (ii) makes the manslaughter of every male defendant NOTHING unless both callers are changed to read the person's sex themselves. The offence is `s 237`, which answers NOTHING for the same person, as M1 requires; so does `s 242` for its own "A female person" limit. No other function of this part answers JUST FALSE for a failed subject limit, apart from two "means" definitions that are closed on purpose (M3: the nurse practitioner of `s 241.1` and the official of `s 269.1(2)`), checked by a script over every function with a subject-status input and a residual JUST FALSE arm: the other `commits ... when` definitions (ss 222(1), 222(5), 223(2), 265(1)) have no subject limit, and the offences with an "if" gate of the text's own (ss 215(2), 242, 264(1)) answer JUST FALSE only for that gate (M2) and NOTHING for a failed subject limit. If a lawyer prefers (ii): one first arm in s 233 (`IF NOT the person is a female person THEN NOTHING`), one layer-1 assertion restated, and ss 231 and 234 given the person's sex; no seat case asks for a man.

### Rulings that govern these forks, word for word

The brief states these rulings; each is recorded again here because this range carries `NOTHING-kind` and `FALSE-kind` tags under them. Rulings that govern a site are applied there: periods, "where" and scope limits (`s 199(4)(b)`, `s 215(4)(c)`, `s 241.2(3.1)(i)`, `s 239(2)`); delegated definitions, defences and "until" bars (`s 197(3)`, `s 215(4)(d)`, `s 269.1(3)`); flat negatives (`s 204(3)`, `s 204(5)`, `s 241(2)` to `(5.1)`, `s 227(1)` and `(2)`, `s 228`); E1: a legal effect is gathered by hand only where the plan requires one answer (`s 231 the kind of homicide offence` gathers ss 222(5), 229, 232(1), 233 and 234 into the single `Kind of homicide offence` that PLAN.md says ccc27 asks for, and `s 235(1)` reads it: option B, by hand, no pilot); everywhere else answers are per section (option C), and nothing here gathers a legal effect by bare name (option A is withdrawn).

- **E1** (bench `commons-negation-1030`; should a legal effect be one definition gathered from every section that gives it?): option B, as guidance for gathering a legal effect's routes by hand only, with no pilot run; option A (gathering by bare name) is withdrawn as a direction for the language, not deferred; option C (per-section answers) applies everywhere else; the Magna Carta pilot in the printed recommendation (chapters 2, 3, 4, 5, 37, 43, 46 and 53, with a blind second reader) is not to be run. The maintainer's lean, verbatim: "B but generally uncomfortable with this much magic because it takes the language from unopinionated to opinionated in ways that are hard to teach an l4 novice." the maintainer's note, verbatim: "Fundamentally I feel like we are trying to make the language do work that the human l4 drafter should do. Too much magic. Can’t we just decline to be this opinionated and say the programmer needs to do this thinking?" Marked 2026-11-03T23:19:02.812Z, ruled 2026-11-04. Bench: https://claude.ai/artifact/49q6k2H5xtuJqy5ttkwZ23
- **N2** (bench `commons-negation-1030`; does a flat negative count as the text's no?): option A as printed: flat negatives count as a no, and where the text names the bearer the prohibition (`SHANT`) is derived from that answer; the Hohfeld kinds stay in each row's "Jural relations" table; option C's wording (the kind typed in the person-facing answer) comes only after a lawyer confirms the typing, decided together with the S/E choice for how "not covered" is said. The maintainer's lean, verbatim: "Why are we even trying to do this work? Some of these rules are metatextual and require more homoiconicity than we currently have in L4." the maintainer's note: none written. Marked 2026-11-03T23:22:49.971Z, ruled 2026-11-04. Bench: https://claude.ai/artifact/49q6k2H5xtuJqy5ttkwZ23
- **Periods, "where" and scope limits** (a question raised on 2026-11-05 while checking an encoding of the Retirement and Re-employment Act 1993, not a bench): (a) a period the text fixes in its own words ("within one month", "begins from ... until the employee attains the age", "on the last day of employment"): outside the period the answer is `JUST FALSE`; (b) a "where ..." condition: when it fails the answer is `JUST FALSE`; (c) a scope limit with no "only" (for example "below" the minimum age): outside the scope the answer is `NOTHING`. The maintainer's words, verbatim apart from the trigger word that ended the message: "i agree with your lean: no for a and b; nothing for c." Typed 2026-11-05T04:30:00.322Z, ruled 2026-11-05.
- **Delegated definitions, defences and "until" bars** (questions raised on 2026-11-05 while checking encodings of the Proceeds of Crime Act 2002, the Terrorism Act 2000 and the Singapore Sale of Goods Act 1979, not a bench): (a) a definition the text itself hands to a list ("Schedule 9 has effect for determining what is a business in the regulated sector"): when no head in the list holds, the answer is `JUST FALSE`; a bare list with no such hand-off still answers `NOTHING`; (b) "it is a defence ... to prove that X": when X is not proved, the answer is `JUST FALSE` for that provision's defence, worded "this section gives no defence on these facts" and never "no defence at all"; (c) "when", "upon" and "whenever" count as "where" under rule (b) of the periods ruling above; (d) "the property does not pass until X": encode the bar ("the property cannot yet pass": `JUST TRUE` before X, `JUST FALSE` after X) and never claim that the property has passed. The maintainer approved these four readings as put to him by the general-manager session, in the word NOTHINGBURGER; his note on (d), verbatim apart from the bracket and the trigger word that ended the message: "in the case of Q5 [the "until" bar] i hope we are using the deontic mechanisms to examine a trace." The note is a request about how (d) is modelled, not part of the ruling. Typed 2026-11-05T06:04:54.553Z, ruled 2026-11-05.
- **Questions with no convention: decide case by case (the maintainer, typed 2026-11-05T10:25:24Z).** Six questions the rulings above leave open are decided site by site, at encoding time, on the text's own words and the encoder's best judgment: an "unless" or "except" once it is met; a bare "must" not done; "listed in Schedule N"; a duty whose "if" or "where" is unmet; "(other than X)"; a missing event in the state ledger. Each such site gets one fork line (the provision, the function, the answer taken, the other reading) for the human reviewer to overrule, and the rulings above still govern whatever they cover. The maintainer's words, asked of the session "Encodings": "re Encoding Conventions I believe I had a short discussion with the coordinator where I basically said I agreed with its \"lean\"s -- does that mean we can retire the bench card or should I go over it still? In short I feel like these decisions need to be made case-by-case at encoding time, and you should go with your gut most of the time; and if the encoding turns out to be wrong, well, that's where the human review comes in." The bench that carried the six questions ("Encoding Conventions", artifact Hpg2k9HqExJN5zdtBYiJ72, collection `encodings-conventions-1105`) was retired unmarked, so it holds no marks.

Decisions made here without a ruling, each marked "assumed, not ruled" where it is written: the seat's handling of section 787(1) (Seams); the `subsection` string `"(1)(a)"` for paragraph 264.1(1)(a); asking another part's answer as one yes-or-no fact in the seat; the marginal readings M1 to M6 below.

## Jural relations (ccc8)

Hohfeld's four pairs, for the person in the seat (the accused) and the state (the Crown, a court, a justice, a peace officer, a Minister): a right (claim) correlates with a duty, a privilege (liberty) with a no-right, a power with a liability, an immunity with a disability.
Per ruling N2 (option A, kept in this table), a flat negative counts as the text's no in the encoding, and where the text names the bearer the prohibition is derived from that answer; the Hohfeld kind of each provision is recorded here and not typed in the person-facing answers.
An offence section ("Every one who X is guilty of ...") is read as: the state has a power to convict and punish (the person is liable), and everyone is under an implicit duty not to do X (the state's claim).
It is recorded once here for all offence sections: ss 201(1), 201(2), 202(1), 203, 204(10), 206(1), 206(4), 207(3), 207.1(3), 209, 213(1), 213(1.1), 215(2), 218, 220, 221, 235, 236(1), 237, 238(1), 239(1), 240, 240.1, 241(1), 241.3, 241.31(4), 241.31(5), 241.4, 242, 243, 244, 244.1, 244.2, 245(1), 246, 247, 248, 262, 263(3), 263.1(1), 264(1), 264.1, 266, 267, 268, 269, 269.1(1).
The table lists the other provisions.

| provision | the relation | who holds it, against whom | note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| s 197(1) definitions (bet, common betting house, common gaming house, disorderly house, game, gaming equipment, keeper, place, public place) | none of its own | the terms scope ss 197 to 213 | definitions only; they decide who bears the duties and liabilities below |
| s 197(2) | immunity | the occupier of a place used by a genuine social club (on the conditions stated) against the state, whose disability is to treat it as a common gaming house under paragraph (a) or (b)(ii) or (iii) | "A place is not a common gaming house ... while ..." |
| s 197(3) | duty (a burden of proof) | the accused, owed to the Crown, to prove that the place is within the exception in subsection (2) | the onus is "on the accused": `s 197(3) the party who bears the onus ...` answers `the accused` |
| s 197(4) | no-immunity (liability) | the keeper, whose place stays a common gaming house although part of the game is played elsewhere or the place is used once | "does not prevent" |
| s 199(1) | power | a justice, over the occupier and persons at a place named in an information on oath (their liability: search, and custody of those found) | for offences under ss 201, 202, 203, 206, 207 |
| s 199(2) | power; duty | a peace officer may take into custody without a warrant a person found keeping or in a common gaming house, and may seize evidence (liability of the person and the thing); the officer must bring them before a justice (the officer's duty, the person's claim) | regulative rule `s 199(2) the peace officer's duty` |
| s 199(3), (5) | power | a court, judge or justice may declare a seized thing forfeited; the Attorney General may then dispose of it (liability of the owner), unless a person shows sufficient cause | cause shown is a defeasible immunity of the claimant |
| s 199(4) | disability then power | no declaration may be made before the conditions of paragraphs (a) and (b) are met (thirty days: fork F1) | the owner's immunity until then |
| s 199(6), (7) | disability | section 199 does not authorize seizure of equipment that other law (s 489) governs | scope limits |
| s 204(1), (1.1) | immunity; privilege | the custodian of stakes, private bettors, and pari-mutuel bettors on the stated conditions, against the state's power to convict under ss 201 and 202; a person may do what ss 201 and 202 describe for legal pari-mutuel betting | exemptions |

## Marginal readings for a lawyer (ccc8)

These are readings of the FALSE and NOTHING convention that a lawyer may overrule; each is a choice between two answers for the same facts, and the one taken is written at every site in a `-- NOTHING-kind` or `-- FALSE-kind` tag.
(The convention is that of 2026-10-29 and the rulings of 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05 quoted in the fork register; the readings below fill the places those rulings leave open.)

- **M1. An offence whose element is unmet answers NOTHING, not JUST FALSE.** "Every person who X is guilty of ..." is an offence definition. When X is not met the answer is `NOTHING` (limited by its own wording): the section says who is guilty, and says nothing of anyone else. The other reading is `JUST FALSE` ("you do not commit this offence"), which would make every offence section a closed list of its own conduct. Where it matters: every `... the person commits the offence ...` function in this range. A caller must read NOTHING as "this section does not reach you on these facts", never as "no offence". One function differs, on purpose: s 233 (M8).
- **M2. An unmet "if", "when", "where", "while" or "until" condition answers JUST FALSE.** Where the text states a condition in its own words ("is, if the child is permanently injured ... guilty of", "when it is planned and deliberate", "where the court is satisfied ... shall give reasons") and the condition fails, the answer is `JUST FALSE` (rulings of 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05, periods and "where"). The other reading is `NOTHING`. Where it matters: for example `s 242 the female person commits the offence ...` (child neither injured nor dead), `s 231(2)`, `s 264(5)`, `s 265(4)`. This applies to a function named for what the provision itself asks (an offence, a bar, a duty, a definition); a function named for a grant or a power is M7.
- **M3. A "means" definition that also says "includes" is read as closed.** "bet means ... and, without restricting the generality of the foregoing, includes ..." (s 197(1)) and "three-card monte means the game commonly known as three-card monte and includes any other game that is similar to it" (s 206(2)) answer `JUST FALSE` for anything else, because the word "means" comes first. The other reading is `NOTHING` (an "includes" list is not closed). Where it matters: `s 197(1) bet`, `s 206(2) three-card monte`.
- **M4. A bare "includes" definition answers NOTHING outside its list.** "keeper includes", "place includes", "public place includes", "abandon or expose includes", "guardian includes", "form of marriage includes", "vehicle includes", "a person's safety includes", "'wounds' or 'maims' includes" and the keel's "intimate partner includes" answer `NOTHING` for a case not named. This is the keel's convention applied unchanged. The consequence worth reading twice: a person in "some other relationship" to the accused is not "not an intimate partner", the answer is NOTHING, so `s 231(5.1)`, `s 236(2)` and `s 263.1(1)` answer NOTHING for them.
- **M5. A "must" that is not done answers JUST FALSE on the "requirements met" function (fork F7).** A bare "must" says what a practitioner has to do and not what follows; the decision function answers JUST FALSE when a requirement is not met, and the regulative rule carries the duty. The other reading is NOTHING. See the fork register.
- **M6. A defence or exception answers JUST FALSE only where the text says no.** For example s 269.1(3) ("It is no defence to a charge under this section that ...") answers `JUST FALSE` for the two circumstances it names and `NOTHING` for any other.
- **M7. A function named for a grant or a power answers NOTHING, not JUST FALSE, when an "if" gate fails (name-test sweep of 2026-11-12, test 1; forks F12, F13 and F14).** `JUST FALSE` on a function named "the Minister may designate ..." or "the practitioner may administer ..." would read as "may not". So `s 204(9.1)` (F12), `s 241.2(3.2)` (F13) and the "if" paragraphs of `s 241.2(3.5)` (F14) answer NOTHING when an "if" condition fails; the other reading is JUST FALSE under M2 (a failed "if" read as the text's own no). It does not apply where the text closes the gate itself ("only if", "only where", "may not ... unless", a list closed with "only"), to a bar, to a duty, or to a function that asks the provision's own question; those keep JUST FALSE. Grants gated by "where", "when" or "upon" or an equivalent ("in the case of") are not flipped: they are listed in the fork register. Functions named "it is lawful ..." are outside the sweep and keep M2: they are listed by name in the sweep subsection, with the other reading.

- **M8. A definition of a category that other sections consume answers JUST FALSE for a subject outside it, where the offence section answers NOTHING (s 233 against s 237; fork F15).** `s 233 the female person commits infanticide` is the definition of the category "infanticide" that `s 231` and `s 234` need as a definite yes or no; a person who is not a female person answers JUST FALSE, so that a man's culpable homicide that is not murder reaches manslaughter. The other reading is NOTHING (a scope limit with no "only", M1 and ruling (c) of 2026-11-05), at the cost that those two callers answer NOTHING for every male defendant unless they read the person's sex themselves. The offence itself (`s 237`) answers NOTHING for the same person.

### Reading a result: FALSE and NOTHING (ccc8)

Every decision rule in this range answers a `MAYBE BOOLEAN`.
- `JUST TRUE`: on the facts given, the case is within the provision on its own words.
- `JUST FALSE`, a "no": the text says no in its own words. In this range that is: a definition that says "means" and the case is outside it; a condition the text introduces with "if", "when", "where", "while", "until" or "only if" that fails (M2; but a function named for a grant or a power whose "if" fails answers NOTHING, M7); a flat negative ("shall not", "does not apply", "is not"); a period the text fixes in its own words and the case is outside it.
- `NOTHING`, "not covered": the text lists without closing the list ("includes"), leaves a term undefined, is silent, points to something outside the sources (a criminal organization in s 467.1(1), violence in s 3.01, section 787(1)), or limits a grant or prohibition in its own wording and the case falls outside the limit; and, for an offence section, the case where the offence's own element is unmet (M1). Each such site carries the sub-kind in its tag: `silent`, `list not closed`, `includes`, `undefined term`, `points outside the text`, `limited by its own wording`.
- A caller must never turn `NOTHING` into `FALSE`; a function that receives a `MAYBE BOOLEAN` from another section carries NOTHING through rather than reading it as false (the layer-1 tests assert it where I wrote the function so; I did not count those).
- `REFUSE` is not used (the stable binary does not accept it): "not covered" is `NOTHING`, asserted with `EQUALS NOTHING`.

Which failures are loud and which are silent, because the silent ones need the attention:
- **Loud.** A name defined in two modules, a wrong argument count, a constructor name that two enums declare (finding F-4), a type the binary does not know: `l4 check` reports each as an error and the file does not run.
- **Silent.** Reading NOTHING as "no offence" (exit code 0, a wrong answer for the person); a seat question that passes its person's yes on as `JUST TRUE` where the earlier question answered NOTHING (the answer is then more definite than the text); a `#TRACE` whose outcome differs between the two stable binaries (no assertion line moves: it was compared by running both, see Status); an expected value copied from the code's own output (not done here: expected values are written beside each assertion before the run). `l4 run` also exits 0 when an assertion fails, so the numbers `check.sh` prints, not the exit code, are the evidence.

## Seams (ccc8)

Inputs of layer-1 functions that are named for the section that answers them (PLAN.md section 3 rule R4), and answers this part gives to others.
The table after this list is generated from the layer-1 modules (every `GIVEN` line whose name ends `, by s ...`, and the section 787(1) input) by a script that is not shipped; the column "answered by" says whether the answering section is in this range (the caller passes that section's answer in, and the seat builds it) or in the keel or another part (the seat asks one fact).

PLAN.md's own list for ccc8, and whether this part follows it:
- `s 3.01 the offence counts as one in which violence is used, threatened or attempted` (keel): taken as an input of `s 263.1(1) the person commits the offence of violence against an intimate partner`. Followed.
- `s 321 the thing is a document` (ccc12): taken as an input of `s 241.4(4) document`. Followed.
- `s 787(1) the general penalty for an offence punishable on summary conviction` (ccc29): taken as an input of every `... the punishment` function whose offence is punishable on summary conviction. Followed.
- Answers given: `s 197(1) the place is a place; the place is a public place` for ccc10 s 286.1(5); `s 231 the kind of homicide offence` for ccc27 (`Kind of homicide offence`); and, for the offences, `s N the class of the offence` (`Class of offence`) and `s N the punishment` (`LIST OF Punishment`) for ccc1, ccc14, ccc20, ccc21 and ccc26. Followed: every offence section in the range has the triple `... commits the offence`, `... the class of the offence`, `... the punishment`.
- PLAN.md says s 231 "turns on s 2 `peace officer`, `justice system participant`, `criminal organization offence`, `terrorism offence`": s 231(4) turns on "police officer" and so on in its own words (asked as four facts, no section 2 function), s 231(6.1) takes `s 2 criminal organization` (a MAYBE BOOLEAN input), s 231(6.01) takes `s 2 terrorist activity`; `justice system participant`, `criminal organization offence` and `terrorism offence` do not occur in the text of s 231 (s 231(6.2) names section 423.1 by number, tested as a `Provision of the Code`, seam rule R1).

Seams the plan lacks, found here:
- **The seat layer cannot state section 787(1).** The punishment of an offence punishable on summary conviction is another part's answer (a `Punishment` record), which no person can state. The seat's gravity-and-penalty record lists only the punishments the section itself states and carries a flag that section 787(1) also supplies a penalty (assumed, not ruled). The merged seat layer should take ccc29's answer and add it to the list; this part's seat does not.
- **Section 245(3), 227(5) and 241(7) pass section 241.1's terms through.** Each is a one-input function (`the answer of section 241.1 for the term`) so that the three exemptions call one definition; the seat calls section 241.1 and passes its answer in.
- **Earlier offences are named by section number and subsection** (`Provision of the Code`), including "(1)(a)" for paragraph 264.1(1)(a) in `s 269.01(1)` (assumed, not ruled: the keel gives no rule for a paragraph).

## Findings: what the text turns out to say (ccc8)

Each finding was checked against the text or a command when it was written (the check is named).

- **F-1. Section 231(3.1) is shown as repealed by 2026, c. 12, s. 17.** The text shows `(3.1) [Repealed, 2026, c. 12, s. 17]` at lines 4524-4525 (`sed -n 4519,4528p`). It is a coverage row with disposition "repealed or blank in this text" and a constant in `ccc8-repealed.l4`. Nothing here depends on what it said.
- **F-2. PLAN.md counts 98 section headings in this range; the text holds 97 section rows.** `units.py` finds 97 rows of kind `section` in lines 4118-5031, 30 of them repealed, so 67 operative sections; every non-blank line of the range is in exactly one row (the audit in `coverage.py` stops if one is not). I could not find the 98th. The brief governs where PLAN.md differs.
- **F-3. Section 207(4.01) cites "paragraph 4(c)" where "paragraph (4)(c)" is meant.** Line 4340: `(4.01) In paragraph 4(c), slot machine means ...`. The encoding reads it as paragraph (4)(c) (`s 207(4.01) slot machine`).
- **F-4. The keel declares one constructor name in two enumerations.** `a person in a capacity that is none of those` is a constructor of both `s 2 peace officer capacity` and `s 2 public officer capacity` in `ccc-definitions.l4` (`grep -c` finds it four times). The stable binary then cannot resolve the bare name ("There are multiple definitions for the identifier"), so no seat or test can pass either of those two constructors. The seat questions that take these capacities (`Under section 199(2)`, `Under section 269.1(1)`) and their cases therefore use other constructors for the "not covered" case, and the two constructors are recorded in NEEDS-CORE.md for the lead to rename.
- **F-5. Section 263.1(3) has no band for an included offence whose maximum is below two years.** Bands (a) to (d) start at "two years or more". An included offence with a maximum of one year, or two years less a day, falls in no band: `s 263.1(3) the term of imprisonment of which the person is liable` answers `NOTHING` for it (tests in `ccc8-tests-part8-harm.l4` and `ccc8-cases-seat.l4`, `Under section 263.1(3)`). "Less than five years" and the other upper limits are read exactly: five years less a day is below five years.
- **F-6. Sections 249 to 261 are all shown as repealed by 2018, c. 21, s. 14.** The 22 rows (lines 4876-4897, checked with `grep -c`) each show `[Repealed, 2018, c. 21, s. 14]`; the impaired-driving offences they held are not in this consolidation at those numbers.
- **F-7. Sections 202 and 203 are indictable only; section 201(1) is hybrid and 201(2) summary only.** The seat cases for section 202(2) and 203 were first written on the assumption that, like section 201(1), they could be prosecuted summarily; the text says "is guilty of an indictable offence and liable" (s 202(2) at line 4212, s 203 at line 4221) and has no summary limb. The first draft of those two expected values was wrong and was corrected before the cases were run; the corrected values come from the text.
- **F-8. In section 215(2), "fails without lawful excuse" is part of the offence's own wording; the "if" conditions that follow are separate.** Under the convention that makes a failed condition JUST FALSE (M2) and an unmet offence element NOTHING (M1), a lawful excuse answers NOTHING and a failure without excuse but outside the "if (i) ... or (ii) ..." conditions answers JUST FALSE. The first draft of one seat expectation said JUST FALSE for a lawful excuse; it misapplied M1 and was corrected before the cases were run again.
- **F-9. Section 2 says "intimate partner ... includes", so the person in "some other relationship" is not covered.** The same first draft of a seat expectation in section 231(5.1) said JUST FALSE for such a victim; the keel's function answers NOTHING, which is what the text's "includes" licenses, and the expectation was corrected before the next run. (See M4.)
- **F-10. The sentencing rules for repeat offences use convictions, not offences (ss 239(2)-(3), 244(3)-(4), 244.2(4)-(5)).** Subsections (3), (4) and (5) say so in terms ("the only question to be considered is the sequence of convictions"); sections 202 and 203, which say "first offence", "second offence", say nothing, hence fork F2.

## What the stable binary does not accept

Observed while writing these files on the stable build (l4 1.6.110, main, e51181d); where the skills describe a construct that the binary rejects, the binary governs.
Each item is an error the binary reported, not a rule read elsewhere.

- `REFUSE`, `#ASSERT REFUSED` and `BECAUSE` (except `LEST BREACH ... BECAUSE`): not used; "not covered" is `NOTHING`.
- `NOT a AND b` parses as `NOT (a AND b)`: every `NOT` is parenthesised.
- A `#ASSERT` must be on one line. A `DEONTIC` cannot be compared with `EQUALS`, so assertions are made on a boolean twin of each regulative rule and the rule itself is run with `#TRACE`.
- A constructor pattern binds positionally: `WHEN `imprisonment for a term of` term THEN ...` is accepted, `WHEN `imprisonment for a term of` WITH `the term` THEN ...` is an "unexpected WITH" error.
- `LIST Punishment WITH ...` is a parse error; a record is built positionally with `Punishment OF ...` and a list with `LIST a, b`.
- `CONSIDER ... WHEN JUST x` as a pattern on a `MAYBE` value was replaced by `BRANCH IF ... EQUALS JUST ... THEN ...` arms; a `CONSIDER` written inside parentheses as a function argument reported an error at the `CONSIDER` (seat, while drafting), and moving it to a `WHERE` binding was accepted.
- There is no type `TEXT`: a string input is `STRING`.
- A name declared in two imported modules (here a constructor in two keel enumerations, finding F-4) "cannot be used at all": the error is "There are multiple definitions for the identifier".
- Dates: `Date day month year` with `IMPORT daydate` (no `YMD`), and `add years` / `add months` from daydate for the periods of ss 215(4)(c), 239(2), 244(3) and 244.2(4).
- `#TRACE` outcomes are not seen by `check.sh`; each trace in the tests was run with both stable builds and the two outputs were compared in full (`diff` of the complete `l4 run` output, 0 differing lines for every test file with a trace).
- The strict `srcrefs.py` gate rejects a `REFN`-style narrower citation placed after a different narrower citation; a block cited by the quote lines directly above it passes.

## What the text does not answer

- Whether a "first", "second" or "subsequent" offence (ss 202(2), 203) counts convictions or offences committed (fork F2); ss 239(3), 244(4) and 244.2(5) answer it for their own sections only.
- Whether a person who is not a female person is outside section 233 (NOTHING) or answers JUST FALSE, as taken (F15, M8): the answer decides whether a man's culpable homicide that is not murder reaches manslaughter in `s 231` and `s 234`.
- Whether a failed "if" on a function named for a grant or a power leaves the grant unmade (NOTHING, taken for F12, F13 and the "if" paragraphs of F14) or is the text's own no (JUST FALSE); and whether a failed "where", "when" or "in the case of" does (JUST FALSE, kept for F11 and the lead-in of F14, which are listed for a lawyer); the same question for the eight functions named "it is lawful" (JUST FALSE kept).
- Whether "within five nautical miles" includes exactly five (F5), how long "one month" and "thirty days" run (F6, F1), whether the ten years of ss 239(2), 244(3) and 244.2(4) end on the anniversary or the day after (F10), and what a bare "must" means for a practitioner who does not (F7).
- Whether the lists the text leaves open ("keeper includes", "place includes", "public place includes", "abandon or expose includes", "guardian includes", "form of marriage includes", "vehicle includes", "intimate partner includes", "wounds or maims includes") are closed: each answers NOTHING outside its list (M4), and the answer for a person in some other relationship to the accused is therefore NOTHING.
- The penalty on summary conviction of every offence that states none: it is in section 787(1), outside this range (Seams).
- What "harassing", "wanton or reckless disregard", "reasonable grounds", "sufficient to deprive an ordinary person of the power of self-control", "more than merely transient or trifling" and similar standards decide on the facts: they are taken as plain inputs, never judged.
- Which proceedings are "proceedings over which Parliament has jurisdiction" (s 269.1(4)) and whether a statement "obtained as a result of" the offence is so obtained: plain inputs.
- Anything in the French text, or in the Interpretation Act and the sections of Part XXIII and section 787, which settle several of the above and are not among this part's ranges.
